PROLOGUE: The Uncertain Thing

Henderson's Will was not as emphatic as Dad's had been. But he made his point nonetheless. As the squidgy little executor dribbled legalese like a leaky faucet, he took the opportunity in a pause nested deeply in [Q: what kinds of clauses?] sub-sub-sub-clauses, to flip one of his Willie Nelson braids off of his lapel. It fell promptly back.

Jake's reflection in my glasses chewed the corners of his nails just like when we were twelve. When we were like brothers. I wondered how he was doing at untangling the tight macarme words, whether he he still habitually grasped at this or that piece for a yarn of his own. Or had the years schooled him? Through loves and divorces, and at least one war-adjacent tour. Would he still spit the chewed bits down his shirtsleeve indiscree--ah, there it is. Same old Jake. Memories of things we'd never acknowledge. Maybe not even to ourselves.

As the executor closed, and the last will and testament of Henderson Patrick Wigglin returned to its paper silence, the Uncertain Thing--the thing that had Jake on edge--became quite certain. To me, at least. To me.

GUARD WITH WONDER This work would probably have been written during the Early Transition (c. 1450–1600), at a time when Sorbo-Gothic merchants and canal workers were establishing their trade network in the West. 1847, Whaling boat on the New England coast, As a child of seven, Cope sees whales and the captian goes to get a harpoon, but they all swim away. His father steered him towards farming or ship-owning, but with a strong natural bent, making him grow into a man of science. Taught names and characteristics of trees and plants, as well as their proper care on their eight acre farm. Encouraged to observe the habits of farmyard beasts, and to keep pets. Trained in accurate map making. Learned this division of animals: Pigs have bristles, Cows have hair, Birds have feathers, Snakes are bare. Was permitted to use his father’s library as he grew older, reading a natural history of Carolina, Florida, and the Bahamas. When he was six, he visited a museum in Philadelphia (either Barnum’s or the Academy of Natural Sciences). He saw the mastodon and the chimeric Hydrarchos/Zeuglodon “the water king” (now Basilosaurus spp).

1861-1862, University of Pennsylvania, Cope studies comparative anatomy under anatomist/paleontologist Joseph Leidy. Learns French & German for scientific readings. Publishes paper on Salamandridae in 1861.

1863-1864, During the Civil War, Cope’s father sends him to Europe to avoid combat (and to break up an unapproved of relationship. He took the breakup poorly. ), although he is interested in being a field surgeon. [Cope is 23-24] Correspondence with Ferdinand Hayden & Joseph Leidy teaches him of the horrors of field surgery. He records the incident in his notebook, along with a drawing. He’s depressed and bored towards the end of this time (France, Germany, Great Britain, Holland, Belgium, Ireland, Austria, Italy, and Prussia -- Visited the collections at Berlin, Leyden, Munich, Vienna, Paris, and London). Upon seeing Archaeopteryx, he wrote to his father that it was intermediate between reptiles and birds, nearer the latter, echoing Huxley. Leidy and Spencer Baird (famous ornithologist & herpetologist) send him along with introduction letters. He meets OC Marsh in the winter of 1863 at the University of Berlin (note: Include German style fencing). A semi-suicidal Cope burned many of his notes and letters from Europe when he returned to Philadelphia. A friend intervenes and saves important material. [Tsiolkovsky was 7-8]. Italy had just unified in 1861, Darwin had published Origin in 1859. Russia finishes emancipating the serfs in 1861. Crimean War in 1854. Austria’s domination of the German Confederacy is coming to a close as, in 1862, under Wilhelm I, Chancellor Otto von Bismarck begins his realpolitik “blood and iron” policy of unifying Germany under Prussian rule, and starts Dano-Prussian War to reclaim Schleswig in 1864. Germany’s advanced and decentralized web of passenger & freight railways connect most markets and settlements in the core regions, costing less than 18pf per ton per km to ship (Bavarian Ludwig Railway). "The great questions of the time will not be resolved by speeches and majority decisions—that was the great mistake of 1848 and 1849—but by iron and blood." Cope relied on Lamarckian ideas and attacked Darwin’s apparent randomness. "The company of passengers is rather uninteresting...I have played chess till I am sick of it...it takes too much time and effort. Play is play & work is work." HE could have witnessed the Plongeur (Diver), the first powered submarine. (Looks a lot like Nimo’s)
1860s - Cope explores the Eastern Seaboard and as far inland as Indiana, especially caves. He visited marl pits.

1865: After he returns to Pennsylvania (Civil War just finished), in a Quaker ceremony, he marries Miss Annie, daughter of Mr. Andrew Pim of Chester Co. PA -- his distant cousin. Their only child, Julia was born the following year.

1867 - Visits Agassiz at Cambridge and tours Brazilian collections

1871 - the Kansas Fossil Fields. Lacking water and good food. Cope suffers “severe attack of nightmare” in whic h”every animal of which he had found trace during the day played with him at night...sometimes he would lose half the night in this exhausting slumber.” Workday lasted from sunrise to sunset.

1872 - 1876, he visits Titanothere Beds of Colorado, and joins the Wheeler survey, unpaid but able to publish, etc. Travels through NM Puerco bluffs, and brings his wife and daughter, renting a house at Fort Bridger, Wyoming. Camped at Ham’s Fork, Wyoming. Bitter Creek, Wyoming (east of Rock Springs). Dog Creek, Missouri?, Cow Island, whence no wagon had ever rolled down such steep hillsides. Mr. Isaac removed everything except the Professor (Cope)’s trunk in order to descend, since it could not be packed on a horse or carried by hand. Down twelve hundred feet, we cut trees, bridged chasms, and made roads. The teamster refused to go any furhter. Mr. Isaac unhitched the lead horses and climging the wago, urged on the stupid mustangs… inevitably, the wagon slowly began to tip, pulling the ponies over sideways and then the whole outfit, wagon and horses began to roll down the slope...

STYLES:
"Don't Mess with Peg" by James Cronchle

LYRICS
# You Don't Mess Around with Peggs

**[Verse 1]**

Downtown's got its coffee shops
And uptown's got its cafés
But the little bakehouse on Fourth and Main
Belongs to a woman named Peggs.
She's got a stare that can curdle custard
And a voice that can stop a fight
And when the bakers get to talkin'
'Bout who runs that place at night...

**[Chorus]**

You don't try to pour powdered sugar
You don't cook with fridgerate eggs
You don't tug. The cork. From a bottle of bubbly.
And you don't mess around with Peggs.

**[Verse 2]**

Well, a young chef came in from the city
With a diploma and a television grin
Said, "I hear you run a pretty good kitchen
But I can show you how it's really done."
He had a camera crew and a clipboard
And a brand-new set of knives
Said, "I'll make that wedding cake in half the time
And it'll be the best you've seen in your life."

**[Verse 3]**

Well, Peggs said, "Son, that wedding's Sunday
And the cake goes out at dawn.
There's six eggs warming on the counter
And the butter's coming along."
But he went and got his eggs from the icebox
And he dumped the sugar straight
And he grabbed a bottle of champagne
To celebrate before the cake.

**[Chorus]**

You don't try to pour powdered sugar
You don't cook with fridgerate eggs
You don't tug. The cork. From a bottle of bubbly.
And you don't mess around with Peggs.

**[Verse 4]**

Well, the batter broke before the frosting
And the sugar filled the air
You couldn't see the wedding cake
Though it was sittin' right there.
Then the cork went off like a shotgun
And the groom flew off the cake
And a hundred little buttercream roses
Came down like a winter flake.

**[Bridge, half-spoken]**

Well, Peggs didn't shout, and Peggs didn't swear
She just took that pencil from her hair
And she looked at the wreckage, and looked at the boy
And she said, "Now ain't that nice."

Then she handed him a broom and a recipe book
And said, "Take yourself a real good look.
That wedding's still tomorrow, son.
And you're gonna make that cake **twice**."

**[Verse 5]**

Now that young man still works for Peggs
And his television days are through
But he makes a mighty fine buttercream
And a pretty good pastry, too.
And whenever some new kid comes through the door
With a fancy set of plans
He puts down his whisk, looks over his glasses
And says, "There's somethin' you oughta understand."

**[Final Chorus]**

You don't try to pour powdered sugar
You don't cook with fridgerate eggs
You don't tug. The cork. From a bottle of bubbly.
And you don't mess around with Peggs.

**[Spoken outro]**

No, you don't mess around with Peggs.

*Especially when there's a wedding on Sunday.*

[234] USER · 2026-10-19T14:09:23.460Z

From NLM



Here are some of the most memorable lines, witty phrasing, and colorful prose pulled from Harry Warner Jr.’s fanzine history column collection:

---

### **On the Mechanics of Writing**

- **John W. Campbell Jr. (as Don A. Stuart)** contrasting mechanical plotting with natural storytelling:
  > *"Walking robots walk scientifically, based on accurate and detailed analyses of the mechanics of walking, and stalk with the **stiff gracelessness of a forced story**."* *"I walked with the precision, the scientifically exact placement of words and phrases and incident that five or six carefully studied rewritings had built into it, word by word... knowing what I was going to tell, **it told itself smoothly**."*
- **Jack Speer** on language and idle thoughts:
  > *"What this country needs is **more synonyms for badly-worn prepositions**."* *"Idle thought with which to occupy your mind while **waiting for the soup to cool**..."*
- **Julian F. Parr** parodying over-inflated sci-fi jargon:
  > *"...utilising the duration-meter of the Graks... I require the experience of watching their reactory process to our **torvously lethal ministrations**."*

---

### **Convention Costumes & Fannish Character Sketches**

- **Milt Rothman** describing the colorful 1941 convention masquerade:
  > *"E. Everett Evans... A blue and yellow suit with a helmet made of **dozens of feathers pasted on one by one. Horribly hot to wear**."* *"Chet Chosen grew a Christ-like beard and came as a prophet; Cyril Kornbluth looked natural and came as a mad scientist; and Doc Lowndes put a mercurochrome cut around his neck, powder on his face, and eyebrow pencil on his eyes and **was a lovely zombie**."*
- **C.S. Youd (John Christopher)** painting a portrait of fellow writer John Burke:
  > *"Both have reverted to Roman Catholic science, and **constructed the universe about the Betelgeuse of their egos**... He is an example of specialised evolution: **the crustacean writer**."*
- **Don Ellis** skewering hyper-critical young readers (*Sweetness And Light*):
  > *"His gluteus maximus is callused by long hours spent sitting reading Science Fiction and thoughtful Books on Fictionized Science... Protected by **strong armour of Self-esteem and his strength is as the strength of ten because his heart is pure**."*

---

### **Pulp Tropes & Early Ray Bradbury Prose**

- **Harry Warner Jr.** describing vintage pulp covers:
  > *"Hugo had the cover so cluttered up with his name, his signs, his disguises and such that **very little of a brilliant yellow Paul sky could be seen**."* *"In the background can be seen purple coated figures, running for a green spaceship, pursued by red and orange monsters from the stone age. **The only colour Paul and Wonder did not use in those days was black**."*
- **Ray Bradbury** in full dictionary-obsessed youth mode (*Futuria Fantasia*):
  > *"The lights of the city were **comets with tails that pelted from right to left like foaming fireworks**."* *"Trees snaky-rooted things whose prehensile branches bore, instead of leaves, flexible lenses... **excrementory ribbons of thin blue vapour streamed from their topmost appendages**."*

---

### **Fancyclopedia Definitions & Witticisms**

- **Jack Speer & Dick Eney** definitions from the *Fancyclopedia*:&#x20;
  > **Gleap:** *"I never knew a virdous gleap, that did not snortle in its sleep."* **Little Jarvenon:** *"A science-fiction house inhabited in mid-1943 by Suddsy Schwartz and Larry Shaw, and **such visitors as they couldn't get rid of**."* **Psychoanalysis:** *"The Futurians say that various of their number have visited professional psychiatrists at times, and **caused the psychiatrists to seek long vacations**."* **Quibbling:** *"What you accuse your opponent of doing when **it's you that's doing it**."*

---

### **Absurdist Fiction & Micro-Stories**

- **Bob Tucker’s "Lez-ettes"** (two-word micro-chapters):
  > *Chapter 1:* Planet. *Chapter 2:* Ice-age. *Chapter 3:* **“Brrr!”** *Chapter 1:* Amoeba. *Chapter 2:* No fission. *Chapter 3:* **Grue-some.**
- **Bob Tucker** on raising babies for space travel:
  > *"Stuff the tube with inflammable material — flashlight powder will do nicely. Then hand the darling genius a lighted match and sit back to see what happens. Chances are, the child will know all about rocketry before his classmates and never, never ask such foolish questions as: **'But, daddy, how can it push against the nothing in space?'**"*
- **Damon Knight** parodying space exploration pulp (*Via Totem Pole*):
  > *"We drank a piece of toast in honour of Ginerton's quick-witted act. **(Toast is liquid on Jupiter. Ugh.)**"* *"I can’t describe how we felt. **Anyway why should I?**"*

---

### **The Realities of Fanzine Publishing**

- **The staff of *Pluto*** lamenting total burnout:
  > *"Only time to eat one meal every three days... Spivis needs a haircut; Paul is a tottering wreck; P.G. needs a wheelchair; Davis needs Spiritus Frumenti; Sisson needs to be salvaged; **Faye needs to spend a month in the soothing calm atmosphere of a boiler factory**."*
- **Francis T. Laney** reflecting on atomic power and human nature:
  > *"We have a civilisation, so-called, which has shown itself to be incapable of even making an equitable distribution and use of steam power... And now we have atomic power. **Children playing with matches. Cthulhu help us!**"*

---

Would you like to explore a particular era, editor, or feud covered in these columns in deeper detail?



[235] USER · 2026-10-19T14:10:05.496Z

Beyond the humor and fannish antics, Harry Warner Jr.’s collection captures deeply poignant moments—flashes of wartime fear, the loss of friends, reflections on human frailty, and the bittersweet reality of watching long-held dreams actually come true.

Here are the most moving and reflective passages from the text:

---

### **On World War II & the Shadow of Atomic Power**

- **Francis T. Laney** writing just days after the first atomic bomb was dropped in August 1945:
  > *"We have a civilisation, so-called, which has shown itself to be incapable of even making an equitable distribution and use of steam power... A civilisation which curtails its food production while a large proportion of its population is enduring sub-standard conditions of nutriment... **And now we have atomic power. Children playing with matches. Cthulhu help us!**"*
- **Ron Holmes** recalling an air raid during the London Blitz (*Fritz and His Blitz*):
  > *"Leisurely and very horribly it came on, nearer and nearer... A blast of air came shrieking along the road, striking me with breathtaking force... **The awareness of the flash seemed to centre about the base of the skull — where it joins the spinal column...** I was unharmed but slightly dazed, avidly listening to the dead silence which followed in the wake of the bomb."*

---

### **On Childhood, Isolation, and Personal Tragedy**

- **Ray Palmer** reflecting in 1934 on the childhood accident that shaped his life:
  > *"At the age of seven... I jousted with a truck in the middle of the street. The truck won; and landing on my head, folded me up to a permanent height of 4’ 8”. I’m still folded. Followed years and years in hospitals. Passed the time reading thousands of books... Sharpest memories of this period were: **the ghastly face of a dead room-mate staring up into the full moon from where he had fallen on the floor in a flood of blood**..."*
- **Francis T. Laney** offering a gentle, empathetic portrait of Myrtle Douglas ("Morojo"):
  > *"She has led a fairly tough life, has been married and divorced twice, and the scrabble of raising a strapping son and supporting herself has left her singularly without the ability to enjoy herself freely and casually... And beneath that occasionally austere facade, **there is one of the most kind hearted persons in Los Angeles... She is the first person most of the older members think of when they are in trouble, and in this selfish civilisation people like that are rare.**"*

---

### **On the Disillusionment of Dreams Coming True**

- **Redd Boggs** reflecting on how reality alters fantasy:
  > *"All of us were alarmed when the atomic bomb fell — alarmed, in part, because it was a horrible weapon, but **equally because it brought fulfillment of an old science fiction dream. Like all dreamers, we science fiction fans enjoy the dream more than the dream-come-true.**"*
- **Harry Warner Jr.** on the eve of the Moon landing, comparing the moment to Christmas Eve as a child:
  > *"I always felt minor pangs of sorrow as each Christmas Eve arrived. Here was the occasion I’d awaited so long, expectations were about to be fulfilled, and yet there was the gnawing, inescapable thought that in a few hours there would no longer be anything to wait and hope for... **I’m a bit unhappy over the knowledge that part of the mystery of the universe is about to be solved, never again to exercise its puzzling fascination for men and fankind.**"*

---

### **Elegies, Vanishing Friends, and Passage of Time**

- **Louis Russell Chauvenet’s** somber poem *Conclusion*:
  > *"Forgive the vain delusion. I have known / At heart how much it angered you that I / Built one strong tower in your sweep of sky / And I will build no more. When viewed alone / The tower seems less strong. **Let stone on stone / Dissolve, and let the bright illusion die.**"*
- **Harold Gottliffe** writing on a changing world:
  > *"No more we’ll sing— for who could fill with praise / Or joy, or gladness the resounding air? / **All this belongs to the unselfish days / Of savage hearts and nights untouched by care.** / Those days are gone, and in their place we find — / Selfish and civilised and racked with pain — / The Era of the Ruled and Ordered Mind..."*
- **Harry Warner Jr.** reflecting on ghost communities and forgotten fans:
  > *"The people who were back of it have vanished completely. I think a virus must have gotten them, although the draft was blamed for a while. **Joseph Gilbert, Harry Jenkins, Lee Eastman, Fred Fischer, Art Sennert, and others — where are they now?**"* *"It would be nice to know whether the winds of the prairie still beat around that little building, whether the files of exchange fanzines have mouldered into dust, and whether in a corner of the building dozens of stiff, dusty inkpads still retain a suggestion of the colours that delighted fandom."*

---

Would you like to focus on the wartime writing specifically, or perhaps explore the personal histories of any particular fans mentioned here?

Trying to make the character sheet that got me kicked out of [https://ursrp.com/](https://ursrp.com/) singable



VEVELEVEV



## Authorial Description

An alien named **VeveleveV** (with up to 112 optional additional syllables) and **[[>[x] Œ [x]<]]** for a title.

The character was intended to be clever and odd in a deliberately difficult-to-parse way: instigating really poorly designed games and then immediately losing them herself, and responding to things through what are basically category errors.

The intended effect was that one might have to read her responses several times and still not be entirely clear whether **her categories don't make sense, or the humans' categories don't make sense**.

She was still in an early stage of development and was intended eventually to become somewhat more legible as a character without ceasing to be alien.

Ok. Workshop time:

They carved the useful bits out of code and held the rest for ransom, handsome on paper, rotten in the bones.  
Called it progress, but it felt like grave-robbing in a blazer, a spreadsheet saint with cemetery tones.  
More labor, less living, bigger billing — that’s the grift, that’s the drift, that’s the line they keep.  
Build the thing, bag the thing, then sell you the hole where the good part used to sleep.  
A system that cashes in on what it cut away ain’t value, it’s a vault with a heartbeat gone dim.  
The workers make the machine; the owners move the empty space and invoice you for the trim.  
They paper over the missing with a glossy little slogan and a quarterly grin.  
Strip out the guardrails, call it agility, then blame the crash on the hands you put in.  
Every shortcut gets a boardroom blessing, every bruise gets filed as a feature request.  
They don’t need to own the work if they can own the gate and charge for access.  
And the worst part is they know exactly what they stole — they just dressed it up as clean.  
Left us with the wrapper and a bill for the bite, like that was the whole machine.

But I was in the room, nodding like I didn’t see the seam  
I signed my name under a deck full of holes and called it lean  
Now the lights are out and the pitch sounds thin in my own mouth  
I helped them strip the wire, then wondered why it went south  
So cut me loose from the polished line, I’m done selling the theft  
Done smiling for the churn while the floor gets dragged from left to right  
If there’s a receipt for the damage, print my name in the margin too  
I’m not their clean excuse anymore — I’m the rust they pushed right through

.
The shimmer of the field is steady, and the convoluted paths in the sketch are silent.




.
The path rests in its three layers between the (+) and (-) terminals. The shimmer around the convoluted wire is still, and the e

 solenoids remain at their stations. There is only the quiet geometry of the page.

[BEGIN_SPECIAL[
use_bpm 40

# CLICK COUNTER
live_loop :click do
  tick                          # Advances a counter each loop iteration
  tick_set 1
  sleep 0.25 if one_in(2)
  sleep 0.25  # Basic pulse
end

# PUNCHY DRUMS
live_loop :drums do
  sample :elec_triangle, amp: 0.5 if one_in(6)
  sleep 0.5
  sample :drum_splash_hard, rate: 1.5, amp: 0.5 if one_in(4)
  sleep 0.25
  sample :drum_cymbal_closed, amp: 0.4 if one_in(3)
  sleep 0.25
end

# CLICK-DRIVEN BASS
live_loop :bass, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :fm
  # Map click to filter cutoff and resonance
  cutoff_val = 70 + (ring 10,0,30,20).tick(:filter_offset)
  res_val = 0.5 + (ring 0,0.2,0.4,0.6).look(:filter_offset) if one_in(3)
  
  with_fx :lpf, cutoff: cutoff_val, res: res_val do
    play choose([:e2, :g2, :b2, :a2]) + rrand(-0.1,0.1), release: 0.5, depth: 2, divisor: 0.8, amp: 0.7 if one_in(2)
  end
  
  # Sub layer with click-controlled amplitude
  use_synth :pretty_bell
  amp_val = (ring 0.3,0.5,0.7,1).look(:filter_offset)
  play choose([:e1, :g1]) + rrand(-0.05,0.05), release: 1, amp: amp_val if one_in(2)
  sleep 0.5
end

# -------------------------
# SUPPORTING MELODY / COUNTERPOINT
# -------------------------
live_loop :supporting_melody, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :prophet
  # Define a simple chord progression to outline the harmonic structure
  chords = [chord(:e4, :minor7), chord(:g4, :major7), chord(:a4, :minor7), chord(:b3, :sus4)]
  # Pick one chord per 2 beats, steady timing
  current_chord = chords.tick
  play_chord current_chord, release: 1.5, amp: 0.4
  
  # Add a simple arpeggiated counterpoint on top of the chord
  use_synth :fm
  4.times do
    play current_chord.choose, release: 0.3, amp: 0.25
    sleep 0.5
  end
end


# GLITCHY MELODY WITH CLICK-MAPPED FX
live_loop :melody, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :piano
  # Map click to echo phase and mix
  phase_val = (ring 0.125, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75).tick(:echo_phase)
  mix_val = (ring 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.4).look(:echo_phase)
  
  with_fx :reverb, room: 0.8, mix: 0.3 do
    with_fx :echo, phase: phase_val, mix: mix_val do
      sleep 0.25 if one_in(2)
      sleep 0.25 if one_in(2)
      sleep 0.75 if one_in(10)
      sleep 0.25  # Basic pulse
      play choose([:g4, :b3, :b4, :g4, :c5]) + rrand(-0.11,0.011), release: 0.2, amp: 0.4 if one_in(2)
      play choose([:e4, :g4, :c4, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.12,0.012), release: 0.3, amp: 0.7 if one_in(4)
      play choose([:c4, :c6, :e5, :e4, :g4]) + rrand(-0.11,0.11), release: 0.2, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/8)
      play choose([:g4, :b5, :b4, :g4, :c4]) + rrand(-0.16,0.04), release: 0.3, amp: 0.6 if one_in(6)
      play choose([:e5, :g5, :c5, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.1,0.1), release: 0.2, amp: 0.6 if one_in(tick/32)
      play choose([:c3, :c4, :e3, :e4, :g3]) + rrand(-0.1,0.21), release: 0.8, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/1)
      play choose([:e5, :g5, :c5, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.01,0.01), release: 0.2, amp: 0.1 if one_in(tick/128)
      play choose([:g4, :b3, :b4, :g4, :c5]) + rrand(-0.1,0.1), release: 0.2, amp: 0.4 if one_in(2)
      play choose([:e4, :g4, :c4, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.2,0.02), release: 0.3, amp: 0.7 if one_in(tick/2)
      play choose([:c4, :c6, :e5, :e4, :g4]) + rrand(-0.03,0.03), release: 0.2, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/64)

If the veneer on the batch of technicalities you processed on the night before hadn’t yet succumbed to dry rot, you may have a moment to catch a nap while drafting grant proposals warmth of syncotron, generated by the friction of the crank rod itself that you had to crank wire sleeping just to give your colleague a break whittle relational tables from teak planchets while buffing the spurs out of the punch card holes by grad students who are still learning how to use a tap and die set. And if not, well… you were right back where you started, platting the land for the next day’s land-grant rush, whilst sitting in the peat bog hoping you had consumed enough calories so as to compress just enough for a full slab cut of peat, dried and cured right on your desk, and carefully scored with a pithing needle for divvying out and ceiling and wax to form the raw cores of the assumptions and bound with bailing wire into a rough-hewn technicality, whilst operating the desiccation suction by mouth for the next batch with one hand, and planing your veneer with the full department toolbox, calipers, slide rule, and jeweler’s loup in the other for the next day’s harvest, wishing for spring to roll around and election threshing season to begin, so you could swap your peat-cutters for a graded sieve stack for the TEM pre-sort and the malting of the electrons!
But try telling kids that these days! They wont believe it.

Episode 2: Exhumation in a Crow's Nest-
[infectious groove] Last time, we left it hanging at the turn-when the silence of lockdown cracked open and the scraps started to shift. The moment a junk-haunted apartment, a bathtub full of trays, and a stack of black negatives turned into something else. The birth of a photographer, right there in porcelain and steam. This is how it happened.
Introduction: Every episode of *A Boxful of Light* is its own crooked experiment-objects, stories, accidents, tools bent sideways. We don't tidy it up. We just smerge it all together. And we, the Deep Dive Al hosts— call us Eileen and Joss-are part of that experiment.
A Boxful of Light — Ep. 2
I wasn't looking for a darkroom. I was in a dark room of my own.
And I was just clearing junk, and collecting some of it. Half-employed, fevered, blackout hours blurring into each other.
COVID isolation. Hot water cut. Internet gone. Electricity hanging by a thread. The Crow's Nest stank of breakup-buttons left behind, words scratched into doorframes. I said it was a workshop, but it wasn't. Just epoxy pours of little objects, resin ghosts, weird paintings, auction flotsam, estate trash, busted furniture stacked in corners.
Then-estate pickup. Basement stripped bare except for one door wedged shut by laundry baskets. I shoved them aside. Cracked the door. It exhaled a lungful of stale dust. At first, it looked like nothing. A closet with dot-matrix reams, curtain rods, junk. Then the bones showed through. A real darkroom, sealed in plain sight.
And the haul-
**Chemistry:** Kodak Dektol, D-19, DK-50, XTOL, Ektonol, Microdol-X. Bottles and packets, labels half-faded. Ilford Warmtone concentrate with caps cracked, still sticky.
**Papers:** Ilford MGIV, Kodak Panalure, Polycontrast RC, Unicolor. Boxes marked '79, '89, '93. AZO silver-chloride sheets, Velox gaslight stock.
**Gear:** trays, tanks, tongs, safelights, enlarger spine without organs. Binders of Kodachrome slides-weddings, picnics, the ordinary frozen mid-century.
Iloaded it all into my mom's pickup. Trays rattling, bottles clinking, negatives whispering in the binders. It felt like stealing time.
One envelope snagged me: **"Jack Phillips / Attorney / July '58."
** Inside, a single negative. When I ran it, the image looked less like a photograph than an unearthing. I never showed it. Some thinne halona to the archive of ahnete Rut it laft a hrand avar

///*****///
///SPECIAL_INTERPRETIVE_FRAMEWORK///
[BEGIN_SPECIAL[
use_bpm 40

# CLICK COUNTER
live_loop :click do
  tick                          # Advances a counter each loop iteration
  tick_set 1
  sleep 0.25 if one_in(2)
  sleep 0.25  # Basic pulse
end

# PUNCHY DRUMS
live_loop :drums do
  sample :elec_triangle, amp: 0.5 if one_in(6)
  sleep 0.5
  sample :drum_splash_hard, rate: 1.5, amp: 0.5 if one_in(4)
  sleep 0.25
  sample :drum_cymbal_closed, amp: 0.4 if one_in(3)
  sleep 0.25
end

# CLICK-DRIVEN BASS
live_loop :bass, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :fm
  # Map click to filter cutoff and resonance
  cutoff_val = 70 + (ring 10,0,30,20).tick(:filter_offset)
  res_val = 0.5 + (ring 0,0.2,0.4,0.6).look(:filter_offset) if one_in(3)
  
  with_fx :lpf, cutoff: cutoff_val, res: res_val do
    play choose([:e2, :g2, :b2, :a2]) + rrand(-0.1,0.1), release: 0.5, depth: 2, divisor: 0.8, amp: 0.7 if one_in(2)
  end
  
  # Sub layer with click-controlled amplitude
  use_synth :pretty_bell
  amp_val = (ring 0.3,0.5,0.7,1).look(:filter_offset)
  play choose([:e1, :g1]) + rrand(-0.05,0.05), release: 1, amp: amp_val if one_in(2)
  sleep 0.5
end

# -------------------------
# SUPPORTING MELODY / COUNTERPOINT
# -------------------------
live_loop :supporting_melody, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :prophet
  # Define a simple chord progression to outline the harmonic structure
  chords = [chord(:e4, :minor7), chord(:g4, :major7), chord(:a4, :minor7), chord(:b3, :sus4)]
  # Pick one chord per 2 beats, steady timing
  current_chord = chords.tick
  play_chord current_chord, release: 1.5, amp: 0.4
  
  # Add a simple arpeggiated counterpoint on top of the chord
  use_synth :fm
  4.times do
    play current_chord.choose, release: 0.3, amp: 0.25
    sleep 0.5
  end
end


# GLITCHY MELODY WITH CLICK-MAPPED FX
live_loop :melody, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :piano
  # Map click to echo phase and mix
  phase_val = (ring 0.125, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75).tick(:echo_phase)
  mix_val = (ring 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.4).look(:echo_phase)
  
  with_fx :reverb, room: 0.8, mix: 0.3 do
    with_fx :echo, phase: phase_val, mix: mix_val do
      sleep 0.25 if one_in(2)
      sleep 0.25 if one_in(2)
      sleep 0.75 if one_in(10)
      sleep 0.25  # Basic pulse
      play choose([:g4, :b3, :b4, :g4, :c5]) + rrand(-0.11,0.011), release: 0.2, amp: 0.4 if one_in(2)
      play choose([:e4, :g4, :c4, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.12,0.012), release: 0.3, amp: 0.7 if one_in(4)
      play choose([:c4, :c6, :e5, :e4, :g4]) + rrand(-0.11,0.11), release: 0.2, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/8) 
      play choose([:g4, :b5, :b4, :g4, :c4]) + rrand(-0.16,0.04), release: 0.3, amp: 0.6 if one_in(6)
      play choose([:e5, :g5, :c5, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.1,0.1), release: 0.2, amp: 0.6 if one_in(tick/32)
      play choose([:c3, :c4, :e3, :e4, :g3]) + rrand(-0.1,0.21), release: 0.8, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/1)
      play choose([:e5, :g5, :c5, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.01,0.01), release: 0.2, amp: 0.1 if one_in(tick/128)
      play choose([:g4, :b3, :b4, :g4, :c5]) + rrand(-0.1,0.1), release: 0.2, amp: 0.4 if one_in(2)
      play choose([:e4, :g4, :c4, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.2,0.02), release: 0.3, amp: 0.7 if one_in(tick/2)
      play choose([:c4, :c6, :e5, :e4, :g4]) + rrand(-0.03,0.03), release: 0.2, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/64)
      play choose([:g4, :b5, :b4, :g4, :c4]) + rrand(-0.01,0.01), release: 0.3, amp: 0.6 if one_in(tick/4)
      play choose([:e5, :g5, :c5, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.316,0.316), release: 0.2, amp: 0.6 if one_in(tick/2)
      play choose([:c3, :c4, :e3, :e4, :g3]) + rrand(-0.05,0.05), release: 0.8, amp: 0.3 if one_in(tick/33)
      play choose([:e5, :g5, :c5, :a4, :e5]) + rrand(-0.03,0.03), release: 0.2, amp: 0.1 if one_in(tick/2)
      putc tick
      tick_reset_all if one_in(0)
    end
  end
end

# RANDOM PERCUSSIVE ACCENTS
live_loop :accents, sync: :drums do
  sleep choose([0.5, 1, 1.5])
  sample :elec_twip, amp: 0.5 if one_in(3)
end

# ATMOSPHERIC PAD WITH CLICK-DRIVEN AMPLITUDE
live_loop :pad, sync: :drums do
  use_synth :dtri if one_in(3)
  use_synth :subpulse if one_in(2)
  sleep 8
  sleep 4 if one_in(3)
  amp_val = (ring 0.2,0.3,0.4,0.5).tick(:pad_amp)
  play_chord chord(:e4, :minor), release: 4, amp: amp_val if one_in(2)
end
]]

haunting, wistful, but almost cozy, Quietly subtle but intriguingly engaging, a slow but irresistible build, in the style of Mary Hopkins and múm, ennui, lightly breathy female voices, using very sparse, melodically beautiful ambient glitch textures and pads, with a pretty, minimalist solo played on a child's toy pushbutton recorder, reworking the primary melody in a way that has a satisfying resolution, followed by a wistful trail-off that comes in and out throughout the rest of the outro, ending the song in a quiet single note played on the same plastic pushbutton recorder, Pops and glitches, 44 bpm, ‑jazz, ‑EDM, ‑stage, ‑musical theater, ‑noise



[spoken:] This is the story of Miss Poughkeepsie 1953...

Miss Poughkeepsie 1953
was not a woman.
Nor was she a man—
though she braised a mean rack of lamb
and lined the little ledge
of her wainscoted home
with tiny Franklin Mint statues.

And though she dreamed up
the three-foot orange owl
for the art of macramé—
Miss Poughkeepsie 1954
was no sort of human at all.

She was a jury-rigged collection
of blood-filled pumps,
vulcanized tubes
under a red polka-dotted house frock,
vivifying the severed head
of a hairless pug
in a raven-black wig.

Built decades before
at the behest of Czar Nicholas—
who, in his final breath,
charged the Kremlin’s best
to craft the perfect woman—
she grew surly, rugged,
and leaked new red polka dots.

And though imperfect,
her statuettes askew,
she was voted best promenader
of that year’s awkward strut,
and she bowed with grace
to take the judge’s sash.

The end.

[517] USER · 2026-10-31T00:47:55.104Z

The thing is… we simply have *no* access to anything that even vaguely corresponds to what is usually meant by terms like: reality, ontology, “the thing itself”, or anything one might wish to propose lies “under” our perceptions, nor any possible way in any conceivable universe to identify anything that might constitute anything like “physical causes/triggers” of perceptions. And we can state that with absolute epistemic certainty. Perception is knowability’s beginning and end, and the surface upon which the absolutely ineluctable certainty of the qualities and patterns of those perceptions are ascertained. It is the absolute totality of the space we can talk about in which the existence or relationships/patterns/connectedness of anything at all has any meaning whatsoever. Whether there is anything “behind” the veil of perception is an entirely meaningless proposition—either a particular experiential phenomenon or pattern between perceived phenomena is known with absolute certainty to appear within perception, in which case, it is a member of the class of things known to be experienced, or it is a proposition whose meaning purports to reference something unobservable, in which case it has a referent that is self-defined as precluding any possible connection to or knowledge of from any point of view, and that means it can have no effect, no observation, and thus no possible reasoning for or against its proposal beyond the conceptual space it occupies *within* perception



[518] USER · 2026-10-31T00:50:24.878Z

[520] USER · 2026-10-31T01:45:54.477Z

Let’s look at some genres:

GENECLECT

	•	PluggnB: A soft-focus mix of plugg-style trap, dreamy R&B synths, and airy melodies (featuring artists like Summrs and Yeat).
	•	Dungeon Synth: Lo-fi, synthesizer-driven ambient music inspired by 1990s black metal and medieval fantasy (featuring artists like Erang).
	•	Breakcore/Footwork Jungle: Fast, fractured rave breakbeats combined with Chicago footwork rhythms and heavy jungle bass.
	•	Deconstructed Club: Experimental electronic dance music that breaks down traditional club beats into abstract, textural sound design.
	•	Blackgaze: Heavy black metal guitar walls layered over the dreamy, melodic wash of shoegaze.
	•	Hyperpop-Adjacent: Post-hyperpop hybrids blending pitched vocals, digital drums, and emotional emo or rap influences.
	•	Neo-Microhouse: Minimal, loop-focused house music stripped down to quiet, micro-textural details at a slow tempo.
	•	UK Underground Rap: Gritty British rap defined by distorted 808s, futuristic synths, and dark Gen Z nihilism (featuring artists like EsDeeKid and fakemink).
	•	Sexy Drill: A New York drill subgenre built around instantly recognizable, three-second production tags (pioneered by Cash Cobain).
	•	Baltimore Club-Rap: High-energy tracks fusing syncopated Baltimore club kick patterns with sharp rap cadences.
	•	Neoperreo: Experimental, queer-informed Latin underground reggaeton that stretches traditional perreo rhythms.
	•	Private School Piano: A chilled-out, soulful subgenre of Amapiano featuring live lounge instrumentation and a relaxed tempo (pioneered by Kelvin Momo). [1, 2]
	•	Asian Diasporic Club: High-energy electronic subgenres pulling from regional working-class and queer internet scenes—including Vietnamese Vinahouse, Taiwanese Taike, and Indonesian Funkot/Indobounce. [1]
	•	Jazz House / Jazztronica: A smoother electronic micro-genre making huge waves, seamlessly blending live jazz instrumentation and jazz chords with warm house grooves (spearheaded by artists like Berlioz). [1]
	•	Drift Phonk: An aggressive, high-octane rap/electronic hybrid pushing out of the underground through short-form video culture, defined by heavily distorted Memphis rap vocals,cowbells, and high-energy bass (led by Kordhell and Interworld). [1]
	•	Barber Beats: A hyper-niche, highly relaxed subgenre of Vaporwave that focuses on slowed-down, smoothly edited lounge jazz and trippy elevator music vibes. [1]
	•	Afro House & Desert House: Deeply percussive electronic movements blending tech-house structure with organic West African and South African rhythms, moving from intimate underground club settings into major festival waves. [1, 2]
	•	Vaportrap: A moody internet hip-hop hybrid that uses the nostalgic, hazy, and corporate aesthetics of 1980s/1990s vaporwave but drops heavy, modern trap drum programming right underneath.  [1]
	•	Breakbeat Techno: A chaotic dancefloor hybrid breaking the classic 4-on-the-floor rules by smashing the fast, relentless energy of hard techno into unpredictable, syncopated breakbeat rhythms.
	•	Cyber-Goth Post-Punk: A massive resurgence of tense, atmospheric darkwave that balances post-punk guitar grit with modern, danceable electronic grooves (like Jeff Wilson's project The Secrecy). [1]
	•	Emo Hyperpop: A highly emotional evolution of early hyperpop that replaces the chaotic sugar-rush energy with vulnerable lyrics, raw emo vocal stylings, and intense electronic/techno production. [1, 2]
	•	Deep Minimal / Naíve Computer Music: A total counterpoint to high-BPM club chaos, utilizing deadpan, stripped-back computer rhythms, primitive synth patches, and deep hypnotic basslines designed for ultra-focus. [1, 2]
	•	Ethereal Abyss Wave: Lush, orchestral ambient soundscapes that romance dark gothic tones, heavily drawing inspiration from early 4AD and Cocteau Twins production styles (pioneered by artists like LOR3L3I). [1]
	•	Baile Funk Futures: The international weaponization of classic Brazilian favela funk, chopped and reconstructed with heavy industrial techno elements or slick UK garage rhythms.  [1]
	•	Chiptune / Tracker Revival: A major underground pivot back to hardware constraints, using old-school sound chips (like the Commodore 64’s SID chip) to build complex, modern synth compositions and tracker-based rave loops.
	•	HexD / Bitpop Rap: A heavily distorted cloud rap mutation where tracks are intentionally up-pitched, bitcrushed, and layered with thick, pixelated digital noise to sound like a corrupted video game file.
	•	Dariacore: A chaotic, hyper-referential subgenre of mashup-core that samples early-2000s pop culture, TV intros, and nostalgic pop tracks, smashing them together at breakneck speeds with heavy jersey club kicks.
	•	Gorge: An intense, mountaineering-themed electronic micro-genre originating in Japan, characterized by heavy use of organic rock and stone sounds, syncopated tom-drum patterns, and a strict "use club tracks like a rugged mountain climb" ethos.
	•	Stutter House: A melodic house offshoot defined by vocal chops that are rapidly sliced and gated using rhythmic volume envelopes, creating a shimmering, emotional "stuttering" effect over a warm four-on-the-floor beat.
	•	Krush / Digicore: A DIY internet-rap movement born on Discord, blending elements of plugg, glitchcore, and experimental electronic sound design with extremely raw, unpolished bedroom vocals. 
	•	Dungeon Synth / Comfy Synth: The cozy, lighthearted cousin of traditional dungeon synth, ditching dark medieval castles for lo-fi, nostalgic synthesizer patches that sound like the soundtrack to an imaginary 16-bit farming simulator or children's book.
The crossover between Vocaloid and hyperpop is an incredible space, blending pitch-shifted synthetic vocals with chaotic, futuristic digital production.
Here are the specific sub-genres, cross-over styles, and artists dominating this exact intersection right now:

The Vocaloid-Hyperpop Crossover
	•	Vocaloid-Core / Digicore Vocaloid: A movement where bedroom producers treat Vocaloid software (like Hatsune Miku, Kafu, or Teto) exactly like hyperpop vocalists—running them through heavy autotune, glitchy stutter-effects, and bitcrushers over abrasive trap and jersey club drums.
	•	Glitch-Pop Vocaloid: Fast-paced, high-BPM tracks that abandon traditional J-Pop structures for the sugar-rush synth stabs, sudden beat drops, and metallic sound design of western hyperpop.
	•	Miku-Garage: A slick hybrid pairing smooth, syncopated UK Garage or 2-step rhythms with floating, airy Vocaloid melodies, giving the synthetic voices a distinct underground club bounce. 

Essential Artists & Producers to Check Out
	•	Kikuo: A legendary producer whose hyper-detailed, experimental, and often darkly whimsical production lays the sonic groundwork for modern avant-pop and hyperpop structures.
	•	Mitchie M: Known for hyper-realistic Vocaloid tuning, often placing synthetic vocals into incredibly polished, upbeat dance-pop and funk frameworks that feel like high-budget hyperpop.
	•	PinocchioP: Sharp, satirical lyrics matched with chaotic, fast-paced electronic rock and synthetic pop beats that carry the exact cynical, online energy of the hyperpop scene.
	•	Giga: A mastermind of heavy, aggressive EDM and trap-infused Vocaloid tracks featuring massive basslines and snappy vocal chopping that fit perfectly into any hyperpop DJ set.

Folk music is actively fragmenting into incredible new modern branches as artists weaponize traditional acoustic storytelling against contemporary production tools, internet culture, and ambient electronic design. [1]
Because folk relies heavily on traditional structures and regional storytelling, these new micro-genres twist those exact roots into entirely new sonic spaces:

1. Folktronica & Electro-Folk
Producers are pairing delicate, fingerpicked acoustics with crisp, synthetic glitches, side-chained synths, and lush ambient pads. Instead of replacing the acoustic core, the digital elements are treated like additional organic instruments. [1, 2]
	•	The Sound: Warm acoustic intimacy smashed into shivering, textured electronic score work.
	•	Key Records: Lost Weekend by Phoebe Bridgers, Singing by Gia Margaret, and A New Chapter of Creativity era tracks by Bon Iver. [1, 2]

2. Free Folk / Freak Folk Revival
A massive underground step away from over-polished commercial folk pop, leaning back toward raw, repetitive, and deeply experimental arrangements. It treats traditional instruments with an almost avant-garde or psychedelic mindset, allowing songs to stretch into hypnotic, long-form improvisations. [1]
	•	The Sound: Droning acoustic layers, unusual tunings, tape hiss, and unpredictable, unpolished vocal delivery.
	•	Key Records: La cuchara caliente, la sopa fría by Adios Adios and the experimental textures of Maria BC's Marathon. [1, 2]

3. Chamber Folk & Progressive Folk
This lane merges traditional folk themes with intricate, orchestral, and progressive-rock arrangements. It pulls inspiration from 1960s/70s English fingerpicking and adds rich, dramatic chamber pop instrumentals like harps, clarinets, and complex strings. [1, 2, 3]
	•	The Sound: Sweeping, cinematic, highly technical instrumentation that frames delicate singer-songwriter poetry.
	•	Key Records: A Gospel of Some Sort by Syd Matters, Autosmile by @, and the sweeping arrangements of Beverly Glenn-Copeland. [1, 2, 3]

4. American Primitivism & Modern Avant-Folk
A highly specific underground revival of instrumental-only storytelling. It fuses classical composition and complex country-blues fingerpicking on a solo acoustic guitar, often introducing jarring rhythms or sudden, sharp tone changes. [1]
	•	The Sound: Utterly hypnotic, solo steel-string guitar pieces that feel ancient and deeply modern simultaneously.
	•	Key Scene-Setters: Guitarist Marisa Anderson's contributions to anthology work and the progressive folk structures of Ohzora Kimishima. [1, 2]

The intersection of obscure regional world traditions and modern underground electronic or experimental production is creating some of the most fascinating micro-genres on earth. Modern internet culture and globalized software allow localized, ancient vocal styles and instruments to break completely out of their geographic borders. [1]
Here are the most notable obscure, traditional, and global avant-folk micro-genres making waves right now:

1. Tuvan Cyber-Throat Singing & Ethno-Bass
Producers are pairing Khoomei (traditional Tuvan throat singing involving simultaneous multi-tonal pitches) with heavy, modern experimental bass and trap production. [1]
	•	The Sound: Low, rumbling, guttural vocal drones and organic horsehead fiddles (igil) layered under subterranean sub-bass and futuristic digital synthesis. [1]
	•	Key Artists: Pioneered conceptually by groups like Huun-Huur-Tu, modern mutations are showing up on experimental electronic platforms across Bandcamp. [1]

2. Slowed Cumbia / Cholombiano Rebelde
A highly specific subculture originating in Monterrey, Mexico, where traditional Colombian cumbia records are intentionally slowed down on turntables to create a heavy, hypnotic, and melancholic dance rhythm. [1]
	•	The Sound: Warped, syrupy accordion loops, deeply sluggish percussion, and pitch-shifted traditional chants that sound like a hazy, analog dreamscape. [1]
	•	Cultural Blueprint: This underground movement and its deep focus on cultural preservation is beautifully captured in the acclaimed independent film I'm No Longer Here. [1]

3. Tsiattista Duelling & Mediterranean Avant-Pop
Originating as a Greek-Cypriot poetic tradition, Tsiattista is essentially an ancient, impromptu form of poetic rap-battling performed at feasts and weddings. Left-field bedroom pop and hyperpop artists in the region are starting to sample these fiery vocal "jousts". [1]
	•	The Sound: Sudden, rapid-fire traditional rhyming structures, intense microtonal violin or lute melodies, and unexpected syncopated electronic glitches. [1]

4. Desert Blues / Tamasheq Rock
A deeply hypnotic folk-rock movement forged in the Sahara Desert by the Tuareg people, blending traditional African rhythms, call-and-response vocals, and electric guitars. [1]
	•	The Sound: Trance-inducing, looping guitar riffs that capture the vastness of the desert, mixed with raw, acoustic hand percussion and poetry reflecting exile and resistance.
	•	Key Artists: Legendary groups like Tinariwen and the blazing guitar work of Bombino. [1]

5. Duduk Ambient & Hauntology
The Armenian duduk is a double-reed woodwind instrument carved from apricot wood, dating back over 1,500 years and known for its deeply melancholic, nasal, and human-like tone. Avant-garde ambient and hauntology producers are building entire genres around its haunting frequencies. [1, 2]
	•	The Sound: Piercing, microtonal wind drones floating over dark synthesizer washes, tape hiss, and decaying loops. [1, 2]

6. Funaná Mutation
Originating in Cape Verde, traditional Funaná is an incredibly fast, rhythmically complex genre originally driven by the accordion (gaita) and a scraped metal bar (ferrinho). European and African electronic DJs are now cross-breeding these rapid rhythms with harsh industrial techno. [1]
	•	The Sound: Intense, hyper-speed polyrhythms, rapid accordion stabs, and punishing, metallic sound design.

If you want to strip away the drum machines, synthesizers, and electronic club aesthetics entirely, the underground folk circuit is experiencing a massive shift toward purely acoustic experimentation, raw regional vocal traditions, and radical historical revivals.
These emerging micro-genres and movements rely completely on the physics of acoustic stringed instruments, unamplified vocals, and organic room tones:

1. Radical Drone Folk & Avant-Trad
Instead of using synthesizers to create an atmospheric wall of sound, these artists use traditional acoustic instruments—like the harmonium, shruti box, hurdy-gurdy, and bowed fiddles—to create dense, heavy, and hypnotic drone music. The focus is entirely on the raw grit of the physical instruments, creaking room tones, and unpolished regional vocal deliveries. [1]
	•	The Vibe: Dark, intense, ancient, and entirely acoustic.
	•	Key Collectives: Look into platforms supporting experimental traditional groups like Shovel Dance Collective or the sprawling Irish avant-folk arrangements of Lankum. [1, 2]

2. American Primitivism (Solo Acoustic Avant-Garde)
A highly technical, completely solo instrumental genre where players approach the acoustic steel-string guitar or banjo with the mindset of an avant-garde composer. They pull from 1920s country-blues fingerpicking but inject intense dissonance, repetitive minimalist structures, and complex, atypical time signatures. [1, 2, 3]
	•	The Vibe: Hypnotic, fiercely intricate, and deeply introspective.
	•	Where to Start: Explore modern solo guitarists keeping this acoustic laboratory alive on Bandcamp's Experimental Folk hub. [1]

3. Pure Indigenous & Polyphonic Vocal Revivals
Moving completely away from western pop scales, underground listeners are diving into unamplified, traditional vocal physics. This includes everything from purely acoustic Tuvan & Mongolian Throat Singing (focusing on the mastery of simultaneous multi-tonal vocal pitches over traditional horsehead fiddles) to Slavic and Georgian Polyphonic Singing. [1]
	•	The Vibe: Haunting, resonant, community-driven, and intensely visceral.
	•	Where to Start: Check out communities on Reddit's Music Recommendations discussing the acoustic work of Siberian indigenous groups or traditional archival field recordings. [1]

4. Minimalist Chamber Folk
A delicate branch of modern folk that swaps out the typical guitar-strumming singer-songwriter setup for sparse, classical instrumentation. It relies on close-miked vocals accompanied only by acoustic instruments like the harp, clarinet, harmonium, or a solitary cello, captured with rich tape saturation to prioritize space, breath, and silence. [1, 2, 3, 4]
	•	The Vibe: Quiet, cinematic, skeletal, and fragile.
	•	Key Artists: Listen to the delicate, room-focused arrangements of Gia Margaret or Maria BC.[1, 2]

Stepping back into the 2000s and 2010s reveals remarkable, long-term movements where highly isolated regional folk and classical traditions underwent radical internal evolutions. These were not quick internet micro-genres, but deep-seated, decade-long movements where master musicians dismantled their own heritage from the inside out:

1. New Weird America & "Wyrd" Folk (Early 2000s)
A highly influential underground movement in the US and UK that resurrected forgotten 19th-century Appalachian folk, psych-folk, and outsider acoustic traditions. Instead of playing standard folk structures, artists used open tunings, free improvisation, tape loops, and thrift-store instruments to create a surreal, pastoral sound. [1]
	•	The Sound: Intimate, sometimes childlike acoustic songwriting smashed into sprawling, trance-like drone rituals.
	•	Key Entities: Early Devendra Banhart, Joanna Newsom (and her avant-garde harp arrangements), and the Jewelled Antler Collective. [1]

2. The "Occult" Irish Avant-Trad Wave (2010s)
The Irish traditional music scene experienced a massive internal schism. Musicians who grew up winning formal, rigid traditional competitions began stripping away the clean, tourist-friendly arrangements of the late 20th century. They returned to the dark, bleak reality of historical songs, utilizing punishing acoustic volume, intense dissonance, and extended instrumental techniques.[1]
	•	The Sound: Guttural, unpolished unison singing, scratching fiddles, and low-end drone work that feels incredibly heavy despite being entirely acoustic.
	•	Key Entities: The early work of Lankum (formerly Lynched), The Gloaming, and Radie Peat.

3. Polish "Radical Traditionalism" & Village Music Revival (2000s–2010s)
In Poland, a wave of urban musicians rejected both classical training and standard pop-folk hybrids. They traveled to remote rural villages to locate the last living masters of pre-WWII Polish "mazurka" dance music. They revived lost instruments like the suka (a fiddle played by stopping strings with the fingernails rather than pressing them to the neck) and focused on a raw, highly energetic "white voice" singing style.
	•	The Sound: Blazing-fast, syncopated, three-beat trance rhythms played on squearing fiddles and frame drums, sounding more like acoustic punk than delicate folk.
	•	Key Entities: Warsaw Village Band (Kapela ze Wsi Warszawa) and the Janusz Prusinowski Trio.

4. Anatolian Rock & Psych-Folk Renaissance (2000s–2010s)
While Anatolian Rock originally peaked in 1970s Turkey, the last two decades saw a massive archival and performance renaissance. Younger Turkish musicians began obsessively digging for obscure, regional microtonal folk songs (Türkü) and recreating them. They did this without abandoning the heavy, acoustic bağlama (lute) but pairing it with vintage analog distortion and hypnotic, rhythmic grooves.
	•	The Sound: Winding, hypnotic Middle Eastern microtonal scales colliding with driving rhythm sections and fuzzy, psychedelic instrumentation.
	•	Key Entities: Altın Gün and the revival work of Gaye Su Akyol.

5. Occitan Polyphonic Resistance (2000s)
Occitan is a historical, endangered Romance language spoken in parts of Southern France, Italy, and Spain. Over the past few decades, underground collectives took ancient Occitan poetry and polyphonic vocal traditions and repurposed them as a form of intense cultural and political resistance.
	•	The Sound: Thunderous, completely acoustic mass-vocal harmonies backed only by simple frame drums (tambourines) and handclaps, building a rhythmic wall of human voices.
	•	Key Entities: Lo Còr de la Plana and Gacha Empega.
